# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2 PAGE 3

## A

1. Globally, one billion children nearly half the world’s children live in countries at extreme climate risk. Climate change is a threat multiplier that exacerbates existing vulnerabilities and increases exposure to natural hazards such as floods, droughts, wildfires, heatwaves, storms, and sea-level rise. These impacts threaten to undermine decades of progress towards achieving the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), including those related to child health, nutrition, education, water, sanitation, and hygiene.

2. Component 1 will directly address and respond to the impacts of climate change on the education sector and ensure that children are protected from the worst effects of climate change. This component will support the development of a comprehensive climate change education strategy for the Ministry of Education (MoE) and its implementation in collaboration with other relevant ministries, including the Ministry of Environment, Climate Change, and Disaster Management. The strategy will include the integration of climate change into teacher training curricula, the development of teaching materials, and the promotion of climate-smart schools. It will also support the establishment of school-based climate clubs to engage students in climate action and promote sustainable practices.

3. Component 2 will support Ministries of Education in three countries: Cambodia, South Sudan, and Uganda. The component will provide technical assistance to develop and implement climate change education strategies that are aligned with national priorities and policies. This will include the development of teaching materials, teacher training, and the promotion of climate-smart schools. The component will also support the establishment of school-based climate clubs to engage students in climate action and promote sustainable practices.

4. Component 3 will support the education community to better prepare, respond, and recover from climate-related disasters. This component will focus on building the resilience of educational infrastructure and promoting climate-smart schools. It will include the development of climate-resilient school designs, the integration of climate change into school curricula, and the promotion of sustainable practices in schools. The component will also support the establishment of school-based climate clubs to engage students in climate action and promote sustainable practices.

# B. PROJECT/PROGRAMME INFORMATION

## B.1 Climate context (page: 1989 words, approximately 2 pages)

South Sudan is expected to heat up by around 3°C (minimum) to 5°C (maximum) by the end of this century under RCP4.5 and RCP8.5 scenarios from 2020 to 2100. This will lead to an increase in the frequency, intensity, and duration of heatwaves, droughts, and floods. These changes are expected to have significant impacts on human health, agriculture, water resources, and infrastructure.

### Health
Heat stress is projected to increase slightly, with almost daily average temperatures around 1.286°C and night-time upward trends. This will lead to an increase in the number of heatwaves by about 30 days per year by the end of this century. The frequency of extreme heat events is expected to double, increasing the risk of heat-related illnesses.

### Agriculture
South Sudan will experience a significant warming trend, with mean temperatures rising by around 0.5°C per decade. By mid-century, the number of frost-free days will increase by about 3 days per decade. These changes will disproportionately affect northern regions like Bahr el Ghazal and Upper Nile, where agriculture is the main economic activity.

### Water Resources
Changes in precipitation patterns are expected to lead to a decrease in many areas, affected by improvements in irrigation systems and water management practices. Central and northern regions will see some reductions in temperature of 0.5°C per decade, but the southern regions will face an increase in temperatures of up to 1°C per decade.

### Infrastructure
Changes in precipitation patterns are expected to lead to a decrease in many areas, affected by improvements in irrigation systems and water management practices. Central and northern regions will see some reductions in temperature of 0.5°C per decade, but the southern regions will face an increase in temperatures of up to 1°C per decade.

![Map](image_url)

### Impacts
- **Health**: Increased heat stress and heat-related illnesses.
- **Agriculture**: Reduced crop yields and increased pest and disease outbreaks.
- **Water Resources**: Changes in precipitation patterns affecting water availability for agriculture, domestic use, and hydropower generation.
- **Infrastructure**: Increased risk of flooding and damage to infrastructure due to changes in precipitation patterns.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2 JANE 8

## B

![Graphs](image_url)

### 3.2

For more detail, maps and graphs on projected climate change impacts in South Sudan see Annex 2 Recently updated section.

Cambodia’s climate projections under the SSP5-8.5 scenario from 2010 to 2050 indicate significant changes in rainfall patterns, temperature, and sea levels. These changes will impact agriculture, water management, and public health, with cascading impacts on school-age children.

Average temperatures are projected to increase by 1.6°C from a baseline of 27°C over the next 30 years. This is expected to lead to increased frequency and intensity of heatwaves, which pose risks to children’s health and well-being. Increased rainfall variability will also lead to flooding and drought, affecting children’s access to clean water and sanitation.

Extreme weather events, such as cyclones and floods, are projected to increase in frequency and severity, disrupting education and increasing the risk of disease outbreaks. Children living in areas prone to these events are particularly vulnerable.

Correspondingly, investments in climate-resilient infrastructure, such as schools, health centers, and water systems, will be essential to mitigate these risks. This includes improving access to clean water and sanitation for children, as well as ensuring that schools and health facilities are equipped to handle extreme weather conditions.

### T12

This scenario had positive effects on school-age children, including health status, diseases, education, and nutrition. However, it also had negative impacts on the environment, such as increased greenhouse gas emissions and deforestation. This scenario assumes an increase in non-renewable energy use, which can have long-term environmental consequences.

Investments in renewable energy sources, such as solar and wind power, are essential to mitigate these risks. Additionally, promoting sustainable farming practices and protecting natural habitats can help reduce the impact of climate change on children’s health and well-being.

### Annex 2

This section provides detailed maps and graphs illustrating projected climate impacts in South Sudan. It includes data on temperature changes, rainfall patterns, and sea-level rise, as well as their potential effects on children’s health and education.

## T44
Target's climate projections under the SSP2-4.5 scenario reveal enhanced changes in rainfall, extreme weather events, and temperature patterns due to anthropogenic effects on natural and human systems. These changes will have significant impacts on agriculture, water resources, coastal zones, and biodiversity. The intensity, severity, and frequency of these impacts are expected to increase significantly with climate change. The projected changes include:

- Increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events.
- The intensity varies as what is likely to be higher extremes, with a delay and the long-term average temperature increases by 1.5°C to 2.0°C.
- Increased risk of droughts, heatwaves, and heavy precipitation events.
- Increased risk of flooding, landslides, permafrost variability, and potential water availability and agricultural cycles.

For the Caribbean region, these changes will have significant impacts on agriculture, fisheries, tourism, and other sectors. The projected changes indicate that the region is likely to experience more frequent and severe weather events, which can lead to increased risks of flooding, droughts, and other natural disasters. These changes are expected to have significant impacts on the region's economy, social well-being, and environmental sustainability.

## T47
The frequency of category 3 cyclones is projected to increase slightly, but tropical cyclones remain a significant threat to the Caribbean region. The projected changes in climate will have significant impacts on the region's infrastructure, which can lead to increased risks of flooding, landslides, and other natural disasters. These changes are expected to have significant impacts on the region's economy, social well-being, and environmental sustainability.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.2 PAGE 8

## Impacts on School-Age Children, School Infrastructure, and Learning

The projected climate change for Sub-Saharan Africa under the Tongue model (SSP5-8.5) scenario highlights significant increases in temperature, precipitation variability, and potential mortality linked to heat stress.

### Key Impacts:

- **Health**: Increased exposure to extreme weather events will exacerbate health issues such as diarrhea and respiratory infections, particularly among children.
- **Nutrition**: Malnutrition rates are likely to increase due to reduced food security, impacting both physical growth and cognitive development in children.
- **Education**: School closures and disruptions may occur, affecting learning outcomes for children. Extreme weather events can also disrupt school infrastructure, leading to temporary or permanent closure of schools.

### Specific Effects:

- **Temperature**: Increased frequency and intensity of heatwaves will negatively impact children's health and well-being, particularly in urban areas.
- **Precipitation**: Changes in precipitation patterns may lead to increased flooding, which can damage school infrastructure and disrupt educational activities.
- **Food Security**: Disruptions in agricultural productivity due to climate change will affect food security, leading to malnutrition among children.

### Recommendations:

- **Healthcare**: Strengthen healthcare systems to better manage climate-related health impacts on children.
- **Education**: Implement flexible learning strategies and improve school infrastructure resilience to withstand extreme weather events.
- **Food Security**: Develop climate-resilient agricultural practices to ensure food security for vulnerable populations, including children.

### Conclusion:

Addressing the impacts of climate change on school-age children requires a multi-sectoral approach that includes health, education, and agriculture. By implementing these recommendations, it is possible to mitigate the adverse effects of climate change on children's well-being and educational outcomes.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

According to leading scientists, increased flooding measures will also reduce pollution, particularly in urban areas, and can lead to higher biodiversity and soil health.

### A2. Address all basic outcomes; climate-induced disruptions to school infrastructure, health, and family livelihoods will affect children's ability to learn and remain in school. School closures and other disruptions will affect children's physical and cognitive development. Such disruptions also close off educational opportunities for girls and women, who are the primary caregivers in the home, with some schools reporting an increase e.g., food impacts. Contributing factors include: (i) lack of access to clean water and sanitation; (ii) poor health care services; (iii) inadequate housing; and (iv) lack of access to education.

### Country Climate Impacts

| Country | Climate Impacts | Contributing Non-climate Factors |
|---------|-----------------|----------------------------------|
| Belize, Guatemala, Honduras | Increased frequency and intensity of hurricanes and tropical storms. | - Poverty: Increased access to clean water and sanitation is limited by poverty. <br> - Health: Limited access to health care services. <br> - Housing: Limited access to adequate housing. |
| Costa Rica, El Salvador, Nicaragua, Panama | Increased frequency and intensity of hurricanes and tropical storms. | - Poverty: Increased access to clean water and sanitation is limited by poverty. <br> - Health: Limited access to health care services. <br> - Housing: Limited access to adequate housing. |
| Guatemala, Honduras | Increased frequency and intensity of hurricanes and tropical storms. | - Poverty: Increased access to clean water and sanitation is limited by poverty. <br> - Health: Limited access to health care services. <br> - Housing: Limited access to adequate housing. |

### Targets

- **Biodiversity**
  - **Habitat Protection**
    - Maintain and restore natural habitats.
    - Protect endangered species.
  - **Conservation**
    - Implement conservation measures.

- **Climate Change Mitigation**
  - **Renewable Energy**
    - Promote renewable energy sources.
    - Reduce reliance on fossil fuels.
  - **Energy Efficiency**
    - Improve energy efficiency in industries and homes.
    - Encourage sustainable practices.

### Implementation Temperature

- **Low**
  - Feasibility: Highly achievable with minimal resources.
  - Risk: Low to moderate risk of failure.
  - Timeframe: Short-term implementation.
  - Cost: Low cost, high return on investment.

- **Medium**
  - Feasibility: Moderately achievable with some resources.
  - Risk: Moderate risk of failure.
  - Timeframe: Medium-term implementation.
  - Cost: Moderate cost, moderate return on investment.

### Benefits

- **Environmental**
  - Reduce carbon emissions.
  - Protect biodiversity and ecosystems.
  - Promote sustainable practices.

- **Economic**
  - Create jobs in green industries.
  - Stimulate economic growth through innovation.
  - Improve energy security and independence.

### Green Economy Corridor

- **Food**
  - Promote sustainable agriculture.
  - Support local farmers and food producers.
  - Encourage organic farming practices.

- **Water**
  - Implement water conservation measures.
  - Protect water resources from pollution.
  - Promote efficient water use in industries.

- **Health**
  - Improve public health through clean environments.
  - Reduce health risks associated with pollution.
  - Promote healthy lifestyles and nutrition.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.0

## B

### Benefits to Stakeholders

- **Direct benefits to countries in addressing climate change:**
  - Increased resilience to climate change impacts.
  - Improved access to climate finance.
  - Enhanced capacity for climate action.

- **Benefits to communities and households:**
  - Reduced vulnerability to climate change impacts.
  - Improved livelihoods through enhanced climate resilience.
  - Access to new opportunities for sustainable development.

### Risks and Uncertainties

- **Risks:**
  - Delays in project implementation due to regulatory or administrative issues.
  - Inadequate capacity of implementing entities.
  - Potential for corruption or mismanagement of funds.

- **Uncertainties:**
  - Changes in global climate patterns that may affect the effectiveness of adaptation measures.
  - Political instability or conflict that could disrupt project implementation.
  - Technological advancements that may render certain adaptation measures obsolete.

### Mitigation Measures

- **Risk Management Strategies:**
  - Strengthen governance and accountability mechanisms to prevent corruption.
  - Enhance capacity building programs for implementing entities.
  - Develop contingency plans to address potential disruptions in project implementation.

- **Uncertainty Management:**
  - Regularly update risk assessments to reflect changes in climate patterns and political stability.
  - Foster innovation and flexibility in adaptation measures to adapt to new technologies and circumstances.
  - Maintain strong partnerships with international organizations and other stakeholders to leverage expertise and resources.

### BRACED: Building Resilience and Adaptation to Climate Extremes and Disasters

BRACED aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate extremes and disasters. The program focuses on improving livelihoods, enhancing food security, and strengthening disaster risk management systems in various regions across Africa and Asia.

- **Key Objectives:**
  - Increase access to climate information services.
  - Improve early warning systems for extreme weather events.
  - Enhance community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) capacities.
  - Promote climate-resilient livelihoods and value chains.
  - Strengthen national and regional policies and institutions.

- **Implementation:**
  - BRACED is implemented through a consortium of international and local organizations, working closely with governments and communities to ensure effective and sustainable outcomes.
  - The program utilizes a combination of approaches, including community-based adaptation, policy advocacy, and research to achieve its objectives.

### Conclusion

The Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal outlines a comprehensive approach to addressing climate change impacts and enhancing resilience at various levels. By focusing on direct benefits to countries, communities, and households, while also managing risks and uncertainties, the proposal aims to create lasting positive changes in vulnerable regions. The BRACED program serves as a key component of this effort, working to build resilience and adapt to climate extremes and disasters.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 PAGE 18

## B

### Key to icons used for climate finance and climate change mitigation and adaptation measures

- Technical assistance
- Capacity building
- Policy dialogue
- Awareness raising
- Institutional strengthening
- Monitoring and evaluation
- Co-financing
- Cross-cutting issues (gender, social inclusion, etc.)

### Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new climate finance vehicle within the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It was established to support mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The GCF aims to promote a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway.

#### Technical assistance

Technical assistance is essential for effective action. It includes capacity building, policy dialogue, awareness raising, institutional strengthening, monitoring and evaluation, co-financing, and cross-cutting issues such as gender, social inclusion, etc. Technical assistance can be provided through various channels including:

- Direct funding
- Technical expertise
- Knowledge sharing
- Capacity building programs

#### Do No Significant Harm (DNSH)

Do no significant harm is a principle that ensures climate change mitigation actions do not adversely affect the livelihoods and well-being of people and ecosystems. The GCF aims to achieve this by:

- Ensuring that projects are designed and implemented in a manner that avoids adverse impacts on people and the environment.
- Conducting thorough impact assessments to identify potential risks and develop strategies to mitigate them.

#### Gender and social inclusion

Gender and social inclusion are critical considerations for effective climate action. The GCF aims to promote gender equality and empower women, girls, and marginalized communities by:

- Ensuring that projects are designed and implemented in a manner that is inclusive of all genders.
- Promoting the participation of women and marginalized groups in decision-making processes.

### General principles

The GCF operates on several key principles including:

- Country-driven
- Transparency
- Accountability
- Respect for human rights
- Environmental sustainability
- Gender equality
- Social inclusion

### Figure 1: Key climate finance issues resulting in inefficiency and underachievement

A number of obstacles still need to be tackled, including that:

- Climate finance is not always accessible or affordable.
- National systems are not always well developed.
- Education and awareness levels are low.
- Key actors lack capacity and resources.
- There is a lack of coordination among different stakeholders.

The GCF aims to address these challenges by providing climate finance in a way that is efficient, effective, and sustainable. It will work with other international organizations and governments to promote climate action and support the implementation of the Paris Agreement.

## B.3 Implementation description (from 2009 words, approximately 5 pages)

### B.3.1 Introduction

B.3.1.1 Background

The BIOFUND focuses on enhancing the green results in the education sector and addressing issues and objects from the environment. The BIOFUND is a non-profit organization that aims to support environmental conservation and sustainable development. It works with local communities, governments, and international organizations to promote biodiversity conservation and sustainable use of natural resources.

### B.3.2 Objectives

The main objective of BIOFUND is to enhance the green results in the education sector by addressing issues and objects from the environment. The specific objectives are:

- To support environmental conservation and sustainable development.
- To promote biodiversity conservation and sustainable use of natural resources.
- To work with local communities, governments, and international organizations.

- Support to environmental conservation and sustainable development.
- Promotion of biodiversity conservation and sustainable use of natural resources.
- Collaboration with local communities, governments, and international organizations.

## B

### Objective

To support the objectives of the Paris Agreement, Goals and targets including those with education as a priority in climate change mitigation and adaptation.

### Activity 1.1.1 Facilitate preparation and implementation of climate and education policies, plans, goals, and guidelines

This activity will work with Ministries of Education and the Government and other stakeholders to develop national frameworks for integrating climate change into educational systems. It will also support the development of teaching materials and training programs on climate change adaptation and mitigation (LMEC1).

### Activity 1.1.2 Strengthen coordination on climate change within Ministries of Education and other relevant line ministries

This activity will work with Ministries of Education to strengthen their capacity to coordinate climate change education policies, plans, goals, and guidelines.

### Activity 1.1.3 Facilitate integration of Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR) into existing and new plans with climate-smart assistance

This activity will support the integration of DRR into national and sub-national level in Comoros and South Sudan to be supported to prepare and test climate-smart assistance.

### Activity 1.1.4 Support children to participate in climate change and education policy initiatives

This activity will work with children to support their participation in climate change and education policy initiatives, including through the Global Youth Climate Network (GYCN).

### Output 1.2 Develop facilities at schools and pre-schools

This output will develop facilities at schools and pre-schools, including water and sanitation facilities, school gardens, and School Management Committees.

### Activity 1.2.1 Develop a national standard for climate-resilient school facilities

This activity will work with Ministries of Education to develop a national standard for climate-resilient school facilities.

### Activity 1.2.2 Facilitate school activities, including DRR and climate-smart agriculture

This activity will support the integration of climate change adaptation into schools in Africa. It is also expected that this activity will support the development of teaching materials on climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### Activity 1.4.1 Strengthen school communities' capacities to operate and maintain schools

This activity will work with school communities to strengthen their capacity to operate and maintain schools.

# Activity 3.2.1: Develop guidelines on climate and education for the GCF and the global education sector

Activity 3.2.1 aims to develop guidelines on climate and education for the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and the global education sector. This activity will support the GCF to develop an education strategy that aligns with the objectives of the Paris Agreement, the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), and the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The guidelines will provide a roadmap for integrating climate change education into existing educational frameworks.

The activity will involve several steps:

- Conducting a literature review to identify best practices in climate change education.
- Engaging with stakeholders, including educators, policymakers, and youth organizations, to gather input on the development of the guidelines.
- Developing a draft framework that outlines key principles and recommendations for integrating climate change education into existing educational systems.
- Conducting consultations with stakeholders to refine the draft framework.
- Finalizing the guidelines and disseminating them to relevant stakeholders.

The activity will be implemented in collaboration with BRACE, UNICEF, and other partners. The guidelines will be developed based on best practices and lessons learned from BRACE's work in climate change education.

## Activity 3.2.6: Develop and share strategic communication materials

Activity 3.2.6 aims to develop and share strategic communication materials that support the implementation of a communication plan for the project. The materials will be designed to raise awareness and engage stakeholders, including donors and partners, in the project's objectives and activities.

The activity will involve several steps:

- Conducting a needs assessment to identify key audiences and their information needs.
- Developing a communication strategy that outlines the project's messaging and target audiences.
- Creating a range of communication materials, including brochures, fact sheets, and social media content.
- Translating the materials into multiple languages to reach a broader audience.
- Disseminating the materials through various channels, including websites, email, and social media.

The activity will be implemented in collaboration with BRACE, UNICEF, and other partners. The communication materials will be designed to be visually appealing and easy to understand, with a focus on raising awareness of the project's objectives and activities.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.0 - PAGE 3

## B

### Introduction

The BRACE project in South Butler County and Youngstown aims to achieve climate resilience while ensuring long-term sustainability through a comprehensive approach. This involves enhancing community resilience, reducing greenhouse gas emissions, and promoting sustainable development. The project emphasizes climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies.

### National Policy Alignment

The project aligns with national policies focused on promoting long-term sustainability in South Butler County. BRACE aims to enhance the resilience of communities by implementing green infrastructure projects. Additionally, the project supports the development of renewable energy sources and promotes energy efficiency.

### Local Ownership and Capacity Building

BRACE demonstrates local ownership by involving community members in decision-making processes related to climate change adaptation and mitigation. The project also focuses on capacity building through training and education programs that empower communities to take action against climate change.

### Scaling Best Practices

The project aims to scale best practices across regions, ensuring lasting impact. By sharing knowledge and experiences, the project seeks to replicate successful strategies in other areas. This includes promoting community engagement, enhancing local capacity, and scaling out the Green Climate Fund initiatives. The initiative supports regional cooperation and integration of solar PV systems, which enhance energy efficiency and support sustainable development.

### Conclusion

BRACE in South Butler County and Youngstown exemplifies a comprehensive approach to climate resilience and sustainability. By aligning with national policies, promoting renewable energy sources, and enhancing community capacity, the project sets a precedent for future initiatives aimed at combating climate change.

### 143 BRAACE aims with several Environmental Goals (SDGs): Specifically SDG4: Quality education for all, SDG5: Gender equality and empowerment of women and girls, and SDG7: Sustainable energy for all. These goals are interconnected and crucial to achieving the overall objectives of the project.

#### To Report

143 BRAACE aims to closely aligned through the BRAACE proposal.

### Co-benefit 1: Gender and social inclusion

**BRAACE** is inherently gendered, focusing on women and girls' empowerment in the energy sector. Women who are denied access to resources due to social exclusion face barriers to economic and behavioral change bringing them into the energy sector. The project will address these issues through capacity building, training, and awareness raising.

### Co-benefit 2: Environmental Impact

Through school opening as a reduced emission from deforestation and degradation (REDD) activities, including tree planting and waste efficient systems for cooking, sanitation, and clean water efforts. Supporting local communities to develop and implement these systems will improve their livelihoods through sustainable practices.

### Co-benefit 3: Socially Increased Locally Adjusted Adaptation (SLAA)

Increasing the SLAA agenda working at the local level to increase resilience to climate change. This includes research on the building and improved school safety plans, local data, local participation, and local adaptation planning. The project will work through schools as a climate change mitigation and adaptation mechanism. Grounded in deep and inclusive community engagement, this approach is designed to bring about lasting changes in behavior and practices.

### D1: Need of response was 800 words

The response to BRAACE includes schools, school administrators, parents, teachers, and those living nearby to the project area. The project will be implemented through a participatory approach that involves all stakeholders. This will ensure that the project is sustainable and meets the needs of the community.

The project will be implemented in three phases:

1. **Phase 1: Planning and Preparation**
   - Conducting baseline studies
   - Developing detailed implementation plans

2. **Phase 2: Implementation**
   - Construction of school facilities
   - Training and capacity building for teachers and students

3. **Phase 3: Monitoring and Evaluation**
   - Regular monitoring of project progress
   - Evaluation of project impact

The project will be implemented over a period of three years, with the aim of achieving sustainable development goals.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1: PAGE 40

In order to foster a society that is informed and capable of responding to climate change challenges, the Climate Change Act 2008 requires the Department for Environment, Food and Rural Affairs (DEFRA) to promote education and training on climate change. This includes developing curriculum-based instruction, designing teaching materials, providing continuing professional development for teachers and educators, and supporting communities to adapt to climate change.

## Cambodia

10% of Cambodia is sea level coastal areas among the Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN). The country has a population of 16 million people with an average age of 25 years. Cambodia is one of the most vulnerable countries to climate change impacts, including flooding and droughts. The National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) has identified climate change as a major threat to national security.

### History

Historically, Cambodia ranks first out of 183 countries in UNICEF's Children's Climate Risk Index based on factors such as child mortality, access to essential services, and exposure to environmental hazards. The country is particularly vulnerable to climate-related impacts due to its low-lying delta region, which is subject to flooding and sea-level rise.

### South Sudan

South Sudan is one of the most environmentally challenged countries in the world. The country has a population of 12 million people with an average age of 18 years. South Sudan is highly vulnerable to climate change impacts, including droughts, floods, and food insecurity. The country's infrastructure is poorly developed, making it difficult for communities to adapt to climate change.

### History

Historically, South Sudan has been ranked as one of the most environmentally challenged countries in the world by various environmental organizations. The country's low-lying delta region is particularly vulnerable to sea-level rise and flooding.

## Nepal

Nepal is a landlocked country with a population of 30 million people. The average age of the population is 25 years. Nepal is highly vulnerable to climate change impacts, including glacial melting, flooding, and droughts. The country's infrastructure is poorly developed, making it difficult for communities to adapt to climate change.

### History

Historically, Nepal has been ranked as one of the most environmentally challenged countries in the world by various environmental organizations. The country's high altitude makes it particularly vulnerable to glacial melting and other climate-related impacts.

## Tonga

Tonga is a small island nation with a population of 105,000 people. The average age of the population is 28 years. Tonga is highly vulnerable to climate change impacts, including sea-level rise, flooding, and droughts. The country's infrastructure is poorly developed, making it difficult for communities to adapt to climate change.

### History

Historically, Tonga has been ranked as one of the most environmentally challenged countries in the world by various environmental organizations. The country's low-lying delta region is particularly vulnerable to sea-level rise and flooding.

## References

- United Nations Children's Fund (UNICEF). Children's Climate Risk Index 2021.
- National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) of Cambodia. Climate Change Vulnerability Assessment Report 2020.
- Government of South Sudan. National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA) 2015.
- Ministry of Forests and Soil Conservation, Nepal. National Adaptation Strategy for Agriculture 2020.
- Tonga Meteorological Service. National Climate Change Policy 2019.

### 103. BRACE is designed to intervene in response to the needs of the education sector in South Sudan. Consequently, any efforts to document lessons learned and disseminate good practices will be a welcome addition to the existing body of knowledge on climate change adaptation and mitigation. This will also contribute to the development of evidence-based policies and action plans for climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### Policy Alignment and Benefits

- **South Sudan National Adaptation Plan (NAP):** The project aligns with the NAP and the recently developed Climate Resilience Strategy (CRS). It supports the implementation of the NAP and CRS through the integration of climate-smart agriculture, water management, and disaster risk reduction.
- **Paris Agreement:** The project contributes to the objectives of the Paris Agreement (COP21) 2015 and the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs), which include adaptation measures for climate change resilience.
- **Green Climate Fund (GCF):** BRACE is aligned with GCF's strategic priorities, particularly in relation to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### Alignment with Gender Action Plans and Policies

- **Gender Mainstreaming:** The project will be designed to mainstream gender into the curriculum, teaching methods, and research activities. This includes ensuring that both male and female students have equal access to education and training.
- **Gender Equality Policy:** BRACE will align with South Sudan's National Gender Policy and ensure that all activities are inclusive and non-discriminatory.

### Compatibility and Coherence with Government Policies and Plans

- **National Development Strategy (NDS):** The project is aligned with the NDS, which aims to promote sustainable development through various initiatives.
- **Climate Change Policy:** BRACE will support the implementation of South Sudan's Climate Change Policy by providing relevant training and capacity building.

### Key Partner Engagement and Government Leadership

- **Ministry of General Education and Instruction (MoGEI):** The project will engage with MoGEI to ensure that the curriculum is aligned with national priorities.
- **Ministry of Environment and Forestry:** BRACE will collaborate with this ministry to integrate climate change adaptation measures into the curriculum.

### Monitoring, Evaluation, and Reporting

- **Monitoring and Evaluation (M&E) Framework:** The project will develop an M&E framework to track progress and ensure that objectives are met.
- **Regular Reporting:** BRACE will provide regular reports to stakeholders on the project's progress and impact.

### Communication and Outreach

- **Stakeholder Engagement:** BRACE will engage with various stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and community groups, to ensure that the project is widely supported.
- **Public Awareness Campaigns:** The project will conduct public awareness campaigns to promote climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### Financial Management

- **Budget Allocation:** BRACE will allocate funds for various activities, including curriculum development, training, and capacity building.
- **Financial Reporting:** Regular financial reports will be provided to ensure transparency and accountability.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.1: Page 4

## Ownership and Risk/Revenue Considerations

The BRRKE Project is a public-private partnership that leverages long-term commitments of both governments and international financial institutions to support the development and implementation of five projects. These include considerations with committed capital, local content, and local job creation. The project will be implemented by the Government of South Sudan in collaboration with private sector partners. The BRRKE Project is designed to strengthen the capacity of the Government of South Sudan to implement climate change mitigation and adaptation activities.

### 10. Ownership and Risk/Revenue Considerations

The ownership and risk/revenue considerations for the project design and implementation will be as follows:

- **Ownership**: The Government of South Sudan will own the projects.
- **Risk**: The risks associated with the projects will be shared between the government and private sector partners.
- **Revenue**: The revenue generated from the projects will be shared between the government and private sector partners.

### 10.1 Efficiencies and Efficiencies Index (50 points, approximately 10%)

The BRRKE Project's expected impact will also contribute to the country's overall GDP growth rate. The project's GDP growth contribution is estimated at 2%, which is significant given the current economic conditions in South Sudan. This growth rate is expected to increase if additional investments are made.

### 10.2 Concessionality Justification

The project's concessional financing level for the BRRKE Project is justified based on the following considerations:

- The project will only enhance existing infrastructure and not create new infrastructure.
- The project will be implemented in a manner that minimizes environmental impacts.
- The project will be implemented in a manner that does not displace local communities or increase their vulnerability to climate change.

### 10.3 Budgetary Constraints and Target Counts

Various factors, including budgeting, management, and technical constraints, have been considered in the design of the BRRKE Project:

1. **Capital Expenditure**: The capital expenditure for the project is limited to $5 million.
2. **Operating Expenditure**: The operating expenditure for the project is limited to $3 million per year.
3. **Local Content**: The project will include local content requirements, including local job creation and procurement.
4. **Environmental Impact Assessment**: An environmental impact assessment will be conducted to ensure that the project does not have adverse environmental impacts.

The project's budgetary constraints are designed to maximize the project's impact while minimizing costs. The target counts for the project are as follows:

- **Target Counts**: 1,000 jobs created.
- **Target Beneficiaries**: 5,000 households benefitted.

The project will be implemented in a manner that is consistent with the Government of South Sudan's long-term growth objectives and is designed to support the country's development goals. The project's implementation will be monitored and evaluated to ensure that it meets its target counts and delivers on its expected impacts.

## Present Use of Works to Remain

The public interest for education and similar educational needs will be served by the continued use of these works.

### Community Wind Farm Constraints

The significant close-proximity cumulative impacts of these large wind farms have been identified as a major constraint to future development. The cumulative impact of the multiple wind farm developments in the region has been assessed using the [Wind Farm Cumulative Impact Assessment](https://example.com) methodology. This assessment has highlighted that the cumulative impacts of these projects will be significant and will require mitigation measures to be implemented.

### EA Modeling Results

The current EA was prepared to assess the cumulative impact of each of the five large wind farms in economic terms for two periods: (1) the average of direct benefits on electricity averages and (2) the average of indirect benefits from the use of these works. The results indicate that the cumulative impacts of these projects will be significant and will require mitigation measures to be implemented.

The EA modeling was conducted over a period using the [BRACE project](https://example.com). The results were calculated over a period using the BRACE project. The results per country have been seen in the table below:

| HCP 1.5 Scenario | Analytical Timeframe: 15 years, Discounted rate |
|------------------|-----------------------------------------------|
| ENPV (US$)       | 9.87 AAAL (US$)                               |

| HCP 2.0 Scenario | Analytical Timeframe: 15 years, Discounted rate |
|------------------|-----------------------------------------------|
| ENPV (US$)       | 30%                                         |

### Application of the Best Practices

The BRACE project is designed to assess cumulative impacts and benefits using a comprehensive and integrated approach. Using a bottom-up and top-down approach, but applying it to the integrated methodologies and the entire economy.

**The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a United Nations-established fund to support projects, programs, policies, and other activities in developing countries that will limit or reduce greenhouse gas emissions and/or adapt to the impacts of climate change.**

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 | PAGE 56

![Green Climate Fund Logo](https://example.com/logo.png)

## Project Title: Renewable Energy Initiative in Canada

In Canada, we propose to implement renewable energy projects in various provinces. The primary goal is to reduce carbon emissions by 30% within the next five years. The following are the key initiatives:

- **Solar Power**: Install solar panels on residential and commercial buildings.
  - **Funding Required**: $15 million
  - **Expected Output**: 2 GW of solar energy

- **Wind Energy**: Develop wind farms in strategic locations.
  - **Funding Required**: $20 million
  - **Expected Output**: 3 GW of wind energy

- **Hydrogen Fuel Cells**: Promote the use of hydrogen fuel cells for transportation.
  - **Funding Required**: $10 million
  - **Expected Output**: 500,000 hydrogen-powered vehicles

In South Sudan, we aim to provide clean water access to remote villages. The project is funded by the Green Climate Fund and focuses on:

- **Water Purification Systems**: Install systems in 20 villages.
  - **Funding Required**: $5 million
  - **Expected Output**: Clean water for 100,000 people

- **Solar-Powered Pumps**: Implement solar-powered water pumps in 10 locations.
  - **Funding Required**: $3 million
  - **Expected Output**: Access to clean water for 50,000 people

In Nigeria, we propose the following initiatives:

- **Afforestation Projects**: Plant trees in deforested areas.
  - **Funding Required**: $8 million
  - **Expected Output**: Reforestation of 10,000 hectares

- **Energy Efficiency Programs**: Promote energy-efficient appliances and practices.
  - **Funding Required**: $7 million
  - **Expected Output**: Reduction in energy consumption by 20%

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 | PAGE 83

![Green Climate Fund Logo](https://example.com/logo.png)

## E

### Where is technology transfer included in the GCF?

Technology transfer is included in the GCF through various initiatives and projects that aim to enhance capacity, promote knowledge sharing, and facilitate access to technologies for developing countries. The GCF supports activities such as:

- **Capacity Building:** Providing training and education to build local capacities.
- **Knowledge Sharing:** Facilitating the exchange of best practices and lessons learned among countries.
- **Access to Technologies:** Helping countries gain access to clean and climate-resilient technologies.

### What are some examples of technology transfer activities?

Some examples of technology transfer activities supported by the GCF include:

- **Renewable Energy Projects:** Promoting solar, wind, and hydro power projects in developing countries.
- **Energy Efficiency Programs:** Implementing energy-efficient solutions for buildings and industries.
- **Climate-Smart Agriculture:** Supporting practices that enhance agricultural productivity while reducing greenhouse gas emissions.

### What are the benefits of technology transfer?

The benefits of technology transfer include:

- **Enhanced Capacity:** Building local expertise to manage and implement climate projects.
- **Economic Growth:** Creating job opportunities and stimulating economic growth through green investments.
- **Environmental Protection:** Reducing greenhouse gas emissions and mitigating climate change impacts.

### How does the GCF support technology transfer?

The GCF supports technology transfer through various funding mechanisms, including:

- **Funding Projects:** Providing financial resources to implement technology transfer projects.
- **Technical Assistance:** Offering technical support and expertise to enhance project outcomes.
- **Partnerships:** Collaborating with international organizations, governments, and private sector entities to leverage resources and expertise.

### What are the key objectives of technology transfer?

The key objectives of technology transfer include:

- **Capacity Development:** Building local capacities to manage climate projects effectively.
- **Knowledge Dissemination:** Sharing best practices and lessons learned among countries.
- **Access to Technologies:** Facilitating access to clean and climate-resilient technologies.

### How does the GCF ensure effective technology transfer?

The GCF ensures effective technology transfer through:

- **Monitoring and Evaluation:** Regularly assessing project outcomes and impact.
- **Capacity Building Programs:** Providing training and education to build local capacities.
- **Partnerships:** Collaborating with international organizations, governments, and private sector entities.

### Project Description

- **Country A**: The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting sustainable urban development. It focuses on improving public transportation and increasing green spaces in cities.
- **Country B**: This initiative seeks to modernize the energy grid by integrating renewable energy sources, reducing reliance on fossil fuels, and enhancing energy efficiency.
- **Country C**: The project supports the transition from traditional energy sources to renewable ones, including solar and wind power. It also aims to create jobs in the green energy sector.

- **Type 1**: Projects that focus on large-scale infrastructure development.
- **Type 2**: Projects that aim to improve energy efficiency and reduce emissions.

The document outlines the allocation of funds under the Green Climate Fund (GCF) for various sectors and regions. The GCF aims to support developing countries in mitigating and adapting to climate change.

- **Sector/Region**: Specific sectors and regions identified for funding.
- **Number of Plans**: Number of plans allocated to each sector or region.
- **10%**: Percentage of funds allocated to the first 10% of plans.
- **100%**: Percentage of funds allocated to all plans.

### 4. Project/programme activities and deliverables

**Output:** A more resilient and sustainable future through improved education, awareness, and capacity building.

**Activities:**

- **Activity 4.1: Output 1.2**
  - **Action:** Conduct a comprehensive assessment of the current climate change education system.
  - **Description:** Identify gaps and areas for improvement in the existing curriculum to better prepare students for future challenges.
  - **Deliverables:**
    - A detailed report on the current state of climate change education.
    - Recommendations for curriculum improvements.

- **Activity 4.2: Output 1.3**
  - **Action:** Develop and implement a national climate change education strategy.
  - **Description:** Create a framework that integrates climate change into various subjects across different educational levels.
  - **Deliverables:**
    - A comprehensive national climate change education strategy document.
    - Training materials for educators.

**Outcome:**

- **Outcome 1: Output 1.2**
  - **Description:** The education sector at national and sub-national levels in targeted countries is more resilient to the impacts of climate change.

**Output:** Enabling systems and processes are put in place and supported.

### Outcome 1

The education sector at national and sub-national levels in targeted countries is more resilient to the impacts of climate change.

### Monitoring, reporting, and evaluation arrangements (max. 380 words)

The GCF will work with accredited entities to develop a monitoring, reporting, and evaluation system that is consistent with the GCF's strategic objectives and the requirements of the Paris Agreement. The system will include indicators for tracking progress towards the GCF's goals and will be designed to support the effective implementation of GCF-funded projects.

process which will evaluate and is connected to existing projects programs against the nature-based solutions and recommended improvements for the next two years of the project.

### M&E The monitoring and evaluation activities will operate related to enabling environment and policy change with qualitative methods and simple quantitative data collection tools. This includes a baseline survey, key informant interviews, focus group discussions, knowledge and attitude surveys, direct observation and whether the project developed learning kits, a digital database for lessons learned and best practices, and other monitoring tools. The M&E will be conducted by local experts and supported by MEAL staff on a quarterly basis.

# E. RISK ASSESSMENT AND MANAGEMENT

## F. Risk factors and mitigation measures (page 3 pages)

### Selected Risk Factor 1 - Low Technical Capacity

| Category | Probability | Impact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Other** | **Description** | **Mitigation Measures** |

- **105**: Limited technical capacity among staff (administrative, technical, and operational) could lead to poor implementation of the project. This could result in delays, cost overruns, and substandard outputs.
  - **Description**: The project requires a comprehensive capacity-building program for government officials, local communities, and other stakeholders to ensure effective implementation.
  - **Mitigation Measures**:
    - Provide training and technical assistance to build capacity among staff.
    - Develop standard operating procedures (SOPs) and guidelines.
    - Conduct regular monitoring and evaluation to identify areas needing improvement.

### Selected Risk Factor 2 - Climate Vulnerability

| Category | Probability | Impact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Other** | **Description** | **Mitigation Measures** |

- **106**: High vulnerability to climate change impacts such as extreme weather events and sea-level rise could delay project implementation.
  - **Description**: The project is located in a region prone to natural disasters, which could disrupt construction activities and affect the timeline.
  - **Mitigation Measures**:
    - Implement climate-resilient design features for infrastructure.
    - Develop contingency plans for unexpected disruptions due to climate events.
    - Conduct regular risk assessments to identify potential vulnerabilities.

### Selected Risk Factor 3 - Fraud and Corruption

| Category | Probability | Impact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **High** | **None** | **Low** |

- **107**: The project is subject to risks of fraud, corruption, and mismanagement.
  - **Description**: The project involves significant financial resources and could be targeted by individuals or groups seeking to exploit vulnerabilities for personal gain.
  - **Mitigation Measures**:
    - Implement robust internal controls and monitoring systems.
    - Ensure transparency and accountability in procurement processes.
    - Conduct regular audits to detect and prevent fraudulent activities.

# Green Climate Fund Proposal V.1.0 | PAGE 183

## Description

The risk of fraud and corruption is broadly recognized and documented processes cost less to the organization than the costs of fraud.

### Key mitigation measures are included in the following sections: internal controls, procurement policies, national institutional studies, and the establishment of useful monitoring and reporting procedures. BRACE will be implemented as a key component of the overall risk management framework. The oversight provided by an independent PIC will ensure that the integrity of the process is maintained. Digital tools can be employed to track expenditures and ensure transparency in the use of funds. A robust procurement framework will be established to manage the control of financial resources, including the development of strong internal controls and procedures, such as the adoption of dual controls.

## Risk Matrix

| Category                | Probability | Impact   |
|-------------------------|-------------|----------|
| Technical and Operational | Low         | Medium   |

### Likelihood (175)

Lack of design input and requisite regulatory budget results in poor quality of design and implementation.

### Mitigation Measures (183)

The risk of fraud will be reduced by implementing the following measures: technical documentation will include the State for Children Regional Office Construction Manual, which provides detailed specifications and drawings. Procurement policies will be implemented to ensure transparency and accountability. The procurement process will be overseen by an independent PIC to ensure that all procurement activities are conducted in accordance with established procedures. All procurement activities will be subject to regular audits to ensure compliance with the procurement manual. The implementation of a robust internal control system will help to prevent fraud and corruption. The use of digital tools for tracking expenditures will enhance transparency and accountability.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 | PAGE 18

## ESS7: Indigenous Peoples

### This conditional loan risk at the highest level was assessed in part because it is likely to be found in the area of projects approved and the countries where they are located.

The World Bank Group (WBG) has a long history of working with indigenous peoples. The WBG's policy on indigenous peoples was issued in 2005, and the WBG has been implementing this policy since then. The policy requires that all projects be designed to avoid adverse impacts on indigenous peoples and their lands and resources.

### Sub-activities

1. **Identify** and **map** indigenous communities.
2. **Consult** with these communities to understand their needs, concerns, and priorities.
3. **Develop** and **implement** an action plan to address the identified issues.

There are no project activities which would require this conditional loan risk.

## Gender Equity and Women's Empowerment

### This project includes activities that support women's economic empowerment and promote gender equality from national development plans.

The project will include activities such as:

1. **Training** in business skills.
2. **Access to credit**.
3. **Support for women-owned businesses**.

This loan is conditional on the implementation of these activities, which are designed to promote gender equity and women's empowerment.

## Accountability and Grievance Mechanism

### There is a possibility that implementing partners may not be able to address grievances from indigenous peoples or women effectively.

The ESS requires the implementation of the BRACE mechanism. The BRACE mechanism is designed to ensure accountability and provide a means for addressing grievances. This includes:

1. **Establishing** a grievance redressal system.
2. **Training** implementing partners on how to use this system.
3. **Monitoring** and **evaluating** the effectiveness of the system.

This conditional loan risk is considered to be low because these activities are already included in the project design.

## F

### T7: The Gender Assessment and Gender Action Plan was developed to generate requests from the five countries that are currently implementing projects with GCF resources. This assessment will be used to identify gender-specific needs, challenges, and opportunities for women's empowerment in climate change adaptation and mitigation. The project will also develop a gender-responsive approach to ensure that the projects and activities include considerations to the specific needs of women and girls. The five domains of analysis are:

- Social norms, roles, responsibilities and time use
- Access to and control over resources and assets
- Decision-making processes and power relations
- Gender-based violence and risks
- Women's empowerment and agency

### T8: The project aims to support the development of gender-responsive climate change policies and programs in the five countries. This will include the integration of gender equality considerations into national climate change policies, strategies, and action plans.

### T9: The project will also provide technical assistance to the five countries to develop and implement gender-responsive climate change projects. This will include the provision of training, capacity building, and other forms of support to help the countries achieve their gender equality goals in relation to climate change.

### T10: The project will also support the development of a gender-responsive monitoring and evaluation system for the five countries. This will include the establishment of indicators and methodologies for tracking progress towards gender equality objectives in climate change projects.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 | PAGE 18

## F

The project will be implemented by the State of Tlaxcala, Mexico, through a decentralized agency called the Secretariat of Environment and Natural Resources (SEMARNAT). The project will be implemented in close collaboration with local communities, including indigenous peoples, and will involve the participation of various stakeholders.

### 180 Financial Management Arrangements

The project will be financed by a combination of GCF resources and co-financing from the Government of Mexico. The financial management arrangements for the project will be governed by the relevant laws and regulations of Mexico, including those related to public procurement, environmental and social safeguards, and audit and evaluation.

- **Simple Scan the Children Tlaxcala (DCTON)** is a newly designed technology that has been implemented in several countries. It is a simple and effective way to scan children for signs of abuse or neglect.
- **Integrated State of the Children Tlaxcala (ISCOT)** was developed by the Government of Mexico to provide a comprehensive overview of the well-being of children in the state. The ISCOT provides data on various indicators, including health, education, and social protection.
- **National Strategy for the Care of Children (CONANI)** was created to enhance the capacity of the government to protect children's rights. The strategy focuses on preventing abuse and neglect, as well as providing support to families in need.

### 185 Implementation Arrangements

The project will be implemented by the SEMARNAT, with technical assistance from the Government of Mexico and other relevant stakeholders. The implementation arrangements will include:

- **Project Management Unit (PMU)** - A dedicated team will be established to oversee the implementation of the project.
- **Stakeholder Engagement** - Regular consultations will be held with local communities, including indigenous peoples, to ensure that their needs and concerns are addressed.

### 190 Procurement Arrangements

Procurement for the project will be conducted in accordance with the procurement policies and procedures of Mexico. This includes:

- **Open Bidding** - All procurements will be conducted through an open bidding process.
- **Competitive Bidding** - The Government of Mexico will ensure that all procurements are conducted on a competitive basis.

### 195 Project Management

The project will be managed by the SEMARNAT, with technical assistance from the Government of Mexico and other relevant stakeholders. The project management arrangements will include:

- **Project Steering Committee (PSC)** - A committee will be established to oversee the implementation of the project.
- **Regular Monitoring** - Regular monitoring will be conducted to ensure that the project is on track.

### 200 Project Evaluation

The project will be evaluated by an independent evaluator, in accordance with the evaluation policies and procedures of Mexico. The evaluation will include:

- **Baseline Assessment** - A baseline assessment will be conducted at the start of the project.
- **Mid-term Review** - A mid-term review will be conducted to assess progress.
- **Final Evaluation** - A final evaluation will be conducted at the end of the project.

### 205 Project Completion

The project will be completed within a period of five years, with regular monitoring and evaluation to ensure that it is on track. The project will be completed in accordance with the relevant laws and regulations of Mexico.

- The GoP will ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the GCF Environmental and Social Safeguards Policy, including its annexes.

### Ministry of Environment

#### No. 385/ENV

To: The Green Climate Fund (GCF)

**Preamble:** 31 April 2025

Re: No-objection letter in respect of the funding proposal titled "Building the Climate Resilience of Children and Youth through Environmental Education" submitted by Save the Children Australia.

Dear Sir/Madam,

We refer to the funding proposal titled Building the Climate Resilience of Children and Youth through Environmental Education (hereafter referred to as the Proposal) submitted by Save the Children Australia to the Green Climate Fund (GCF) on 14 October 2024. The Ministry of Environment, the national designated authority and authority of the Kingdom of Cambodia, pursuant to GCF decisions 8/05/PM, 8/37/PM, and 6/45/PM, the content of which we fully endorse, hereby communicate our no-objection to the Proposal.

By communicating our no-objection, it is implied that:

- The Ministry of Environment has reviewed the Proposal and is in conformity with the national priorities, strategies, and plans.
- We also confirm that our national process for considering no-objections to the Proposal has been duly followed.

We encourage Save the Children Australia to take the necessary steps to implement the Proposal in a manner consistent with the GCF environmental and social safeguards. We further encourage any project proponent wishing to apply for funding from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to submit their application to the GCF secretariat.

We acknowledge that the later version of this no-objection letter will be available on the GCF website at [link].

Yours sincerely,

Dear Madam/Sir,

# Secretariat’s assessment of FP274

## Proposal name:
Building the Childcare Benefits of Children and Communities in Cambodia, South Sudan, Tonga

## Accredited entity:
Save the Children Australia

## Countries:
Cambodia, South Sudan, Tonga

## Project size:
Small

## L. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### Strengths
**Building the Childcare Benefits of Children and Communities in Cambodia, South Sudan, Tonga (BRACE)**
- The BRACE initiative is a systematic way to enhance child readiness for education.
- It provides a framework for integrating childcare benefits into education systems.
- The project ensures that children are ready to learn by addressing their needs.
- Schools, teachers, and families benefit from the program’s holistic approach.

### Points of caution
**The sustainability of BRACE’s intervention**
- The initiative relies on external funding to scale recommended activities.
- Financial ability to scale recommended activities is uncertain.
**Budget allocation**
- Budget allocations should prioritize children in South Sudan. Tonga benefits from a relatively stable political environment and has a more significant amount of education budget allocation, making it less dependent on BRACE’s intervention for sustainability.

### Recommendations
**Legion of children may enjoy**
- Children in Cambodia and South Sudan may benefit the most from this initiative.
- The project should focus on these countries to ensure maximum impact.

# Summary of the Secretary’s assessment

## Project Background

1. Climate change poses an existential threat to education systems globally, with one billion children and youth currently living in high-risk settings. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) aims to support developing countries in addressing climate change through mitigation and adaptation measures.
2. The BRACE project will strengthen local development outcomes in GCF-accredited South Sudan and Ethiopia, focusing on climate-resilient education infrastructure. It will enhance the capacity of national and sub-national governments to integrate climate resilience into education systems, WASH impacts, and school community safety management. This will support the implementation of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).
3. The BRACE project will strengthen local development outcomes in GCF-accredited South Sudan and Ethiopia, focusing on climate-resilient education infrastructure. It will enhance the capacity of national and sub-national governments to integrate climate resilience into education systems, WASH impacts, and school community safety management. This will support the implementation of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).
4. The project will achieve climate-resilient education outcomes through school-based approaches.
5. Implementation at scale relies on target countries’ institutional capacities, including climate-resilient education policies, standardised tools, safety systems, and climate-focused curricula.
6. The project will support the development of climate-resilient education infrastructure, including climate-resilient schools, planning adaptation and disaster response, and promoting finance and budgeting for climate resilience.
7. The project will support the development of climate-resilient education infrastructure, including climate-resilient schools, planning adaptation and disaster response, and promoting finance and budgeting for climate resilience.
8. The project will support the development of climate-resilient education infrastructure, including climate-resilient schools, planning adaptation and disaster response, and promoting finance and budgeting for climate resilience.

# Outcome 1

Outcome 1 of the project is designed to enhance the relevance of the education sector in Gambella, South Sudan and Tonga to the lives of current and future climate risks. It aims at developing a climate change teaching and learning module for primary schools and to establish the conditions necessary for long-term, sustainable implementation.

## Activity 1.1

### Activity 1.1.1
Facilitate preparation and understanding of climate and education policies, plans and guidelines.

### Activity 1.1.2
Facilitate integration of climate change within subjects of education at other relevant line ministries at the national and subnational levels.

## Activity 1.2

### Activity 1.2.1
Facilitate integration of disaster risk reduction (DRR) into existing curricula through education departments, and facilitate the development of teaching materials for climate change education policy initiatives.

### Activity 1.2.2
Develop a national standard for climate-resilient school facilities.

### Activity 1.2.3
Retrofit school facilities, including WASH and internet access.

### Activity 1.2.4
Strengthen school communities and education officials to prepare and maintain school infrastructure and facilities.

## Activity 1.3

### Activity 1.3.1
Build capacity of national and subnational education officials on climate change teaching and learning materials.

### Activity 1.3.2
Build knowledge and capacity of school management, teachers and communities on climate change teaching and learning materials.

### Activity 1.3.3
Implement school-level disaster-related school safety plans.

### Activity 1.3.4
Provide climate-resilient tools, equipment and funds to schools.

### Activity 1.3.5
Strengthen access to and use of early warning systems and climate information services by education officials.

## Outcome 2

Outcome 2 aims at climate change resilience teaching and learning methodologies for national curricula.

## Activity 2.1

### Activity 2.1.1
Develop teaching and learning materials on climate change ready to implement through schools, non-formal and informal systems.

### Activity 2.1.2
Build knowledge and capacity of education officials and educators on climate change teaching and learning methodologies.

### Activity 2.1.3
Strengthen the capacity of children's clubs to lead climate change teaching and learning in their communities.

### Activity 2.1.4
Disseminate climate change teaching and learning materials through schools, non-formal and informal systems.

# Population and sustainability potential

The BRACE project aims to address the challenges of education in three target countries by focusing on their respective needs. The project will support the construction of schools and provide educational materials to enhance the quality of education. Additionally, it will promote gender equality and empower girls and women through scholarships and training programs.

## Component 2: "Transforming girls' lives through education"

The BRACE project aims to address the challenges of education in three target countries by focusing on their respective needs. The project will support the construction of schools and provide educational materials to enhance the quality of education. Additionally, it will promote gender equality and empower girls and women through scholarships and training programs.

### Paradigm shift potential

The BRACE project provides an opportunity to create a paradigm shift in school culture and climate by addressing the Comprehensive School Safety Framework (CSSF). This focus on safety, equity, and inclusion can lead to improved learning outcomes for students. The project addresses barriers in governance, gender, and disability, and promotes inclusive education through community engagement and safe learning environments. Additionally, the project aims to integrate climate-resilient practices into school operations.

### United components 3 & 4: "Combating violence against children and young people"

The BRACE project will address the challenges of violence against children and young people by promoting a culture of safety and respect in schools. The project will work with communities, parents, and other stakeholders to create safe and supportive learning environments.

### Sustainable development potential

The BRACE project provides an opportunity to promote gender equality and social inclusion by addressing the needs of marginalized groups. The project aims to empower women and girls through education and training programs, and to promote gender equality in schools and communities.

# Green Education Foundation

serve the community by bringing extensive knowledge to the present prominent natural habitat and preserve beauty for gifts and treasures, enhancing community capacity, increasing WILLIAM water solutions and solving the problems of the environment. The BRAEC project supports communities by promoting DREI principles and practices, including environmental education, sustainable development, and environmental protection. The project also promotes the integration of environmental education into school curricula and teacher training programs.

The BRAEC project also aims to enhance community capacity for environmental management and sustainable development (PPD) through the promotion of natural resource conservation and sustainable use. The project will support communities in developing and implementing local environmental action plans, including school-based environmental practices, waste management systems, and hygiene practices.

The BRAEC project promotes a holistic approach to environmental education by providing schools with the necessary resources and training to integrate environmental education into their curricula. The project also supports teachers in developing and delivering environmental education programs that are relevant and engaging for students.

The BRAEC project is aligned with the following Sustainable Development Goals: 4 (Quality Education), 5 (Gender Equality), 6 (Clean Water and Sanitation), 11 (Sustainable Cities and Communities).

# Target's inclusion prevents faces significant challenges from climate change

Includes sea level rise, droughts, floods, tropical cyclones and heatwaves, with schools, particularly in outer islands, being highly vulnerable to these impacts. The Government has highlighted the need to prepare students for climate-related professions. Past climate events have increased awareness of climate change risks among communities. Finding ways to incentivize teachers to meet the adaptation needs of the disadvantaged would enhance their resilience and capacity to respond to climate change.

## 3.5 Country ownership

The BRAIC project aligns with the national climate change strategies and educational policies of the Government of Tuvalu. It aligns with South Pacific NGO and academically international collaboration on climate change education. The project will support the development of Framework for a long National Action Plan on Climate Change Adaptation and Disaster Risk Reduction (NAP-CCRR) and the development of a National Climate Change Education Strategy. The project is also designed to be gender-responsive and inclusive, addressing the needs of girls, women, people with disabilities, and marginalized groups. It will support the Government Gender Policy, Community Gender Mainstreaming Strategy Plan in Climate Change and Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR), by ensuring equal access to climate change education and DRR, reducing gender inequalities in climate change adaptation and DRR, and empowering girls and women with disabilities in their communities.

In addition, the project will support the Government of Tuvalu's efforts to integrate climate change into the national curriculum. It will also align with the Ministry of Education's efforts to integrate climate change education into the school curriculum and address other climate challenges. The project will provide training for teachers on climate change education and disaster risk reduction (DRR). It will also support the development of teaching materials, including lesson plans and activities that promote climate change adaptation and DRR.

## 3.6 Efficiency and effectiveness

The BRAIC project is expected to directly benefit 5001 students/teachers in 24 schools across Tuvalu through improved climate change education and disaster risk reduction (DRR) outcomes. It targets the most climate vulnerable populations in the education sector, including teachers and students. The project will improve the quality of teaching and learning materials on climate change adaptation and DRR by developing a comprehensive curriculum that is culturally appropriate and relevant to the needs of Tuvalu's students and teachers. The project will also support the development of a national climate change education policy and strategy.

The project's outcomes are designed to be sustainable, with a focus on building the capacity of teachers and schools to continue to deliver climate change education and DRR long after the project has ended.
